F

The diagram commutes because it consists of merely
F
- and
R
- moves. The two equalities in the above diagram both come from the fact that the left hand sides and right hand sides differ merely by moving the worldlines without bending them.

H.1.2
The inverse map of Eq. (
39
)

The above discussion fully specifies Eq. (
39
). Now we construct the inverse map of Eq. (
39
), which shows that Eq. (
39
) is bijective.

Suppose that we start from an SET phase with an internal symmetry
G
, and we know whether, after the inverse correspondence, each
g\in G
becomes a lattice symmetry action (which may preserve or reverse the spatial orientation) or remains internal. Then the explicit form of the inverse map of Eq. (
39
) is as follows.

\displaystyle\rho_{g}(a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a),&\textrm{if $g$ preserves spatial orientation or remains internal,}\\
\rho^{\prime}_{g}(\bar{a}),&\textrm{if $g$ reverses spatial orientation.}\end{array}\right.

(196)

\displaystyle U_{g}(a,b;c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c),&\begin{array}[]{l}\textrm{if $g$ preserves spatial orientation}\\
\textrm{ or remains internal,}\end{array}\\
K^{q^{\prime}(g)}([R^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{a})\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{b})}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{c})}]^{T}[A^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{b})\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{a})}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{c})}]^{T})K^{q^{\prime}(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c),&\begin{array}[]{l}\textrm{if $g$ reverses spatial orientation.}\end{array}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}),&\begin{array}[]{l}\textrm{if $g_{1}$ preserves spatial orientation}\\
\textrm{ or remains internal,}\end{array}\\
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}U^{\prime}_{g_{2}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a}),\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(a);0)K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})},&\begin{array}[]{l}\textrm{if $g_{1}$ reverses spatial orientation}\\
\textrm{ but $g_{2}$ preserves it or remains internal,}\end{array}\\
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}U^{\prime}_{g_{2}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(a),\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a});0)\varkappa^{*}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a})}K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})},&\begin{array}[]{l}\textrm{if both $g_{1}$ and $g_{2}$ reverse spatial orientation.}\end{array}\end{array}\right.

Here, as before, the primed data is from the SET phase with an internal symmetry, and the unprimed data is for the SET phase with a general symmetry.

Now we derive this inverse map. For later convenience, it is useful to introduce the notation

p(g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{if $g$ preserves spatial orientation,}\\
1,&\textrm{if $g$ reverses spatial orientation,}\end{array}\right.

(197)

where
g
is a symmetry in the TQSL with a general symmetry.

First,
\rho_{g}(a)
is simply the inverse function of
\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)
in Eq. (
184
).

For
U_{g}(a,b;c)
, when
g
preserves spatial orientation or remains internal, it is clear that
U_{g}(a,b;c)=U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)
. When
g
reverses spatial orientation after the inverse map is applied, we have, according to the original map,

U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)=K^{q(g)}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c).

(198)

We reverse this map by multiplying the inverse of the factor consisting of
A
and
R
on both sides and re-expressing the symmetry action on anyons using
\rho^{\prime}_{a}(g)
. Note that when
g
reverses spatial orientation, its unitarity is reversed under the map, so
K^{q(g)}OK^{q(g)}=K^{q^{\prime}(g)}O^{*}K^{q^{\prime}(g)}
where
q^{\prime}(g)=q(g)+p(g)
. Consequently, we have

U_{g}(a,b;c)=K^{q^{\prime}(g)}([R^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{a})\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{b})}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{c})}]^{T}[A^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{b})\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{a})}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(\bar{c})}]^{T})K^{q^{\prime}(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c).

(199)

For
\eta_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})
, we use the same treatment to derive the inverse map. When
g
preserves spatial orientation or remains internal, it is clear that
\eta_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})
. When
g_{1}
preserves spatial orientation or remains internal while
g_{2}
reverses spatial orientation, it is straightforward that we have

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q(g_{1})}U^{*}_{g_{2}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g_{1})}

(200)

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}U^{\prime}_{g_{2}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a}),\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(a);0)K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}.

When both
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
reverse spatial orientation, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q(g_{1})}U^{*}_{g_{2}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa_{{}^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g_{1})}

(201)

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}U_{g_{2}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}K^{q^{\prime}(g_{2})}([R^{\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{2}}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta)\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{2}}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a})}_{0}][A^{\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{2}}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a})\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{2}}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta)}_{0}])K^{q^{\prime}(g_{2})}U^{\prime}_{g_{2}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a})}K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}U^{\prime}_{g_{2}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\left[K^{q^{\prime}(g_{2})}\theta^{*}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{2}}}(^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta)}K^{q^{\prime}(g_{2})}\theta_{{}^{\overline{g_{1}}}\hskip-1.0pta}\right]\varkappa^{*}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a})}K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}U^{\prime}_{g_{2}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(a),\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a});0)\varkappa^{*}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\overline{g_{1}}}(\bar{a})}K^{q^{\prime}(g_{1})}.

In the last equality above, we have used the property of topological spin
\theta_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}=K^{p^{\prime}(g)+q^{\prime}(g)}\theta_{a}K^{p^{\prime}(g)+q^{\prime}(g)}
and the fact that
p^{\prime}(g_{2})=0
for any internal symmetry.

Combining all the above, we reach Eq. (
196
), which shows that Eq. (
39
) is bijective.

H.2
Checking the consistency equations

After describing the map Eq. (
184
) and its inverse Eq. (
196
) in Appendix
H.1
, now we check that data obtained from the map Eq. (
184
) indeed satisfies the correct set of consistency equations.

We copy the consistency equations satisfied by the data
\mathcal{C}_{1,\mathbbm{1},1}
for an SET phase with a general symmetry
G
:

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}[F^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd}]_{(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte,\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf,\rho^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})}K^{q(g)}

(202)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu,\nu,\rho,\sigma}[U_{g}(a,b;e)]_{\mu^{\prime}\mu}[U_{g}(e,c;d)]_{\nu^{\prime}\nu}[F^{abc}_{d}]_{(e,\mu,\nu)(f,\rho,\sigma)}[(U_{g}(b,c;f)^{-1})]_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}[(U_{g}(a,f;d))^{-1}]_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}},

(203)

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}[J_{g}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})]_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}K^{q(g)}=\sum_{\mu\nu}[U_{g}(b,a;c)]_{\mu^{\prime}\mu}[R^{ab}_{c}]_{\mu\nu}[U_{g}(a,b;c)]_{\nu\nu^{\prime}}^{-1},

(204)

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu}K^{q(g)}[U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)]_{\mu\nu}K^{q(g)}[U_{g}(a,b;c)]_{\nu\rho}=\frac{\eta_{c}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{b}(g,h)}[U_{gh}(a,b;c)]_{\mu\rho},

(205)

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(g)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)=\eta_{a}(gh,k)\eta_{a}(g,h).

(206)

Our goal is to prove that, if the unprimed data in Eq. (
184
) satisfies Eqs. (
203
), (
204
), (
205
) and (
206
), then the primed data in Eq. (
184
) satisfies the following equations:

\displaystyle K^{q^{\prime}(g)}(F^{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(b)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(c)}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(d))})_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(e)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(f)}K^{q^{\prime}(g)}=U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;e)U^{\prime}_{g}(e,c;d)(F^{abc}_{d})_{ef}U^{\prime}_{g}(b,c;f)^{-1}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,f;d)^{-1},

(207)

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{g}(b,a;c)R^{ab}_{c}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)^{-1}=K^{q^{\prime}(g)}(R^{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(b)}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(c)})K^{q^{\prime}(g)},

(208)

\displaystyle K^{q^{\prime}(g)}U^{\prime}_{h}(\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a),\rho^{\prime}_{g}(b);\rho^{\prime}_{g}(c))K^{q^{\prime}(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)=\frac{\eta_{c}^{\prime}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}^{\prime}(g,h)\eta_{b}^{\prime}(g,h)}U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c),

(209)

\displaystyle K^{q^{\prime}(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q^{\prime}(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

(210)

These equations are just the version of Eqs. (
203
), (
204
), (
205
) and (
206
) when there is only internal symmetry, and they must be satisfied by the data
\mathcal{C}_{1,\mathbbm{1},1}
for an SET phase with a purely internal symmetry
G
.

We first prove that
U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)
satisfies Eq. (
207
). When
g
preserves spatial orientation, since
U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)=U_{g}(a,b;c)
, it is straightforward to see that Eq. (
207
) holds. When
g
reverses spatial orientation,

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;e)U^{\prime}_{g}(e,c;d)(F^{abc}_{d})_{ef}U^{\prime}_{g}(b,c;f)^{-1}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,f;d)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte})(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd})(F^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd})_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf})^{-1}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd})^{-1}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte}A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd}(F^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd})_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pte^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf}^{*}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf})^{-1}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptf^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptd})^{-1}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}(F^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{d}})_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{e}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{f}}^{*}K^{q(g)}.

The first equality is achieved by using Eq. (
203
), the second equality is achieved by using Eq. (
195
), and the last equality is achieved by using Eq. (
188
). Summarizing the two cases, we conclude that

K^{q^{\prime}(g)}(F^{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(b)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(c)}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(d))})_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(e)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(f)}K^{q^{\prime}(g)}=U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;e)U^{\prime}_{g}(e,c;d)(F^{abc}_{d})_{ef}U^{\prime}_{g}(b,c;f)^{-1}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,f;d)^{-1},

(211)

which is Eq. (
207
).

We then check Eq. (
208
). When
g
preserves spatial orientation, it is again straightforward to see that Eq. (
208
) holds. When
g
reverses spatial orientation,

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{g}(b,a;c)R^{ab}_{c}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(b,a;c)R^{ab}_{c}U^{-1}_{g}(a,b;c)K^{q(g)}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})^{-1}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})^{-1}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})(R^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})^{-1}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})^{-1}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})^{-1}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})^{-1}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})^{-1}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}})^{*}K^{q(g)}

The first equality is obtained by directly inserting the definition of
U^{\prime}_{g}
, the second equality is obtained by using Eq. (
204
) for an orientation-reversing symmetry, the third equality is a direct evaluation of the third line, and the last equality is obtained by using Eq. (
190
). Summarizing the two cases, we conclude that

K^{q^{\prime}(g)}(R^{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)\rho^{\prime}_{g}(b)}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(c)})K^{q^{\prime}(g)}=U^{\prime}_{g}(b,a;c)R^{ab}_{c}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)^{-1},

(212)

which is Eq. (
208
).

We now check Eq. (
209
). When multiplying
U^{\prime}_{g}
and
U^{\prime}_{h}
together, there are four cases to be considered: 1) both
g
and
h
reverse spatial orientation; 2)
g
reverses spatial orientation while
h
does not; 3)
h
reverses spatial orientation while
g
does not; 4) both
g
and
h
preserves spatial orientation. For the first case, we find that

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}U^{\prime}_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})^{*}K^{q(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}K^{q(h)}(A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}}R^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}})^{*}K^{q(h)}U_{h}^{*}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}(A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}})^{*}K^{q(h)}U^{*}_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}})^{T}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}(A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}})^{*}K^{q(h)}U^{*}_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}})^{T}A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)\frac{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc;0)}{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;0)U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;0)}\frac{\theta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}}{\theta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\theta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}})^{*}A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\frac{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}}{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}}K^{q(g)}\frac{\eta_{c}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{b}(g,h)}U_{gh}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{c}^{\prime}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}^{\prime}(g,h)\eta_{b}^{\prime}(g,h)}U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c).

The first equality is achieved by directly inserting the definition of
U^{\prime}_{g}
and
U^{\prime}_{h}
, and
\overline{gh}=\bar{h}\bar{g}
is the inverse group element of
gh
; the second equality is achieved by using Eq. (
204
) for an orientation-reversing symmetry; the third equality is achieved by using Eq. (
189
); the fourth equality is obtained by using Eq. (
190
) and the ribbon identity
R^{ab}_{c}R^{ba}_{c}=\theta_{c}/(\theta_{a}\theta_{b})
; the fifth equality is achieved by using Eq. (
205
); and the last equality uses the definition of
U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)
and
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)
in Eq. (
184
).

When
g
is orientation-reversing while
h
is not, we have

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}U^{\prime}_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})^{*}K^{q(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}U_{h}^{*}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}K^{q(h)}A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc}K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)\frac{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c};0)}{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};0)}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc}(R^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(h)}\frac{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c};0)}{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};0)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc}(R^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(gh)}\times K^{q(g)}\frac{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c};0)}{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};0)}K^{q(g)}\frac{\eta_{c}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{b}(g,h)}U_{gh}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\frac{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c};0)}{U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};0)}K^{q(g)}\frac{\eta_{c}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{b}(g,h)}U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{c}^{\prime}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}^{\prime}(g,h)\eta_{b}^{\prime}(g,h)}U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c).

The first equality is achieved by directly inserting the definition of
U^{\prime}_{g}
; the second equality is achieved by using Eq. (
190
); the third equality is achieved by using Eq. (
204
) for an orientation preserving symmetry (note that
h
is orientation preserving here); the fourth equality is obtained by using Eq. (
205
); the fifth equality is achieved by using the definitions of
U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c)
in Eq. (
184
); and the last equality uses the definition of
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)
in Eq. (
184
).

When
h
is orientation-reversing while
g
is not, we find that

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}U^{\prime}_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)^{*}K^{q(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}K^{q(h)}(A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}(A^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(gh)}\frac{\eta_{c}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{b}(g,h)}U_{gh}(a,b;c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{c}^{\prime}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}^{\prime}(g,h)\eta_{b}^{\prime}(g,h)}U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c),

where we have used Eq. (
205
), and the definition of
U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c)
and
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)
in Eq. (
184
).

When
g
and
h
both preserves spatial orientation, the relation between
U_{g}^{\prime}
’s are trivially identical to the relation between
U_{g}
’s. Summarizing the four cases, we have Eq. (
209
):

K^{q(g)+p(g)}U^{\prime}_{h}(\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a),\rho^{\prime}_{g}(b);\rho^{\prime}_{g}(c))K^{q(g)+p(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)=\frac{\eta_{c}^{\prime}(g,h)}{\eta_{a}^{\prime}(g,h)\eta_{b}^{\prime}(g,h)}U^{\prime}_{gh}(a,b;c),

(213)

The final task is to check Eq. (
210
),
where
g
,
h
, and
k
can be either orientation-preserving or orientation-reversing; hence, there are
8
cases to be verified. We first show the cases where all three actions reverse spatial orientation. Note that
hk
and
gh
preserve spatial orientation. Then,

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\left(\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(h,k)K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)(\theta_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}})\right)^{*}K^{q(gh)}\left(\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(g)}U_{hk}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}\right)

Using the relation,

\displaystyle K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)=\frac{1}{\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(h,k)}U_{hk}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0),

(214)

we substitute
\eta^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(h,k)U_{hk}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)
with
K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)
and get

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(g)}\left(K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}\right)^{*}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(g)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(gh)}\theta^{*}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(gh,k)\eta_{a}(g,h)\times K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

The second equality is obtained by massaging the factors; the third equality is achieved by using the properties of the topological spin
\theta_{{}^{g}\hskip-1.0pta}=K^{p(g)+q(g)}\theta_{a}K^{p(g)+q(g)}
and the
\varkappa
-symbols Eq. (
191
); the last equality uses the definition of
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)
and
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)
. As such, when
g
,
h
, and
k
are all orientation-reversing, the corresponding
\eta^{\prime}
phases satisfy the expected equation.

When
g
and
h
reverse spatial orientation while
k
preserves spatial orientation, note that
gh
preserves spatial orientation while
hk
reverses spatial orientation. We confirm that

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta^{*}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(g,h)K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)^{*}K^{q(gh)}\times\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(g)}U_{hk}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)^{*}K^{q(h)}\theta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(g)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)\times K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(gh,k)\eta_{a}(g,h)\times K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

The first equality is obtained by directly plugging in the definition of
\eta^{\prime}
; the second equality uses the relation Eq. (
214
); the third equality is obtained by merely massaging the factors; the fourth equality uses the relation between
\eta
’s; the last equality again uses the definition of
\eta^{\prime}
. As such, when
g
and
h
reverse spatial orientation while
k
preserves spatial orientation, the corresponding
\eta^{\prime}
phases satisfy the expected equation.

When
g
and
k
reverse spatial orientation while
h
preserves spatial orientation, note that
gh
and
hk
both reverse spatial orientation. We verify that

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(h,k)K^{q(g)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(g)}U_{hk}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)\theta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(gh,k)\eta_{a}(g,h)\times K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)K^{q(gh)}\times K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(gh,k)\eta_{a}(g,h)\times K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(gh)}\times K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

The first equality is obtained by directly plugging in the definition of
\eta^{\prime}
; the second equality uses the relation Eq. (
214
); the third equality uses the relation between
\eta
’s; the fourth equality uses the property of
\theta
and
\kappa
; the last equality again uses the definition of
\eta^{\prime}
. As such, when
g
and
k
reverse spatial orientation while
h
preserves spatial orientation, the corresponding
\eta^{\prime}
phases satisfy the expected equation.

When
g
reverses spatial orientation while
h
and
k
preserve spatial orientation, note that
gh
reverses spatial orientation while
hk
preserves spatial orientation. We verify that

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta^{*}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(h,k)K^{q(g)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(g)}U_{hk}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,hk)K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(g)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)\times K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(gh,k)\eta_{a}(g,h)\times K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(gh)}\times K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

The verification is essentially the same as the previous verifications. We thereby confirm that when
g
reverses spatial orientation while
h
and

preserve spatial orientation, the corresponding
\eta^{\prime}
phases satisfy the expected equation.

When
g
preserves spatial orientation while
h
and

both reverse spatial orientation, note that
gh
reverses spatial orientation while
hk
preserves spatial orientation. We check that

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(gh)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{a}(gh,k)\times K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\theta_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}\varkappa^{*}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}K^{q(gh)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

When
g
and
k
preserve spatial orientation while
h
reverses spatial orientation, note that
gh
and
hk
both reverse spatial orientation. We check that

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(h)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(gh)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{a}(gh,k)\times K^{q(gh)}U_{k}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(gh)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

When
g
and
h
preserve spatial orientation, no matter
k
reverses or preserves spatial orientation, we always have

\displaystyle K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q(g)+p(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\eta_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(h,k)K^{q(h)}\eta_{a}(g,hk)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,h)\eta_{a}(gh,k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

Summarizing the eight cases, we have Eq. (
210
):

K^{q^{\prime}(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)}(h,k)K^{q^{\prime}(g)}\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,hk)=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(gh,k)\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h).

(215)

The above discussion shows that the data obtained from the map Eq. (
184
) satisfy the correct set of consistency equations.

H.3
The correspondence of the gauge transformation

In the above, we have explicitly shown that the primed data
U_{g}^{\prime}(a,b;c)
and
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)
satisfy Eqs. (
207
)-(
210
). Now we show that when the original data
U_{g}(a,b;c)
and
\eta_{a}(g,h)
undergo gauge transformation Eqs. (
181
)-(
183
), which are copied below:

\displaystyle[(F^{abc}_{d})_{\{x\}}]_{\{e,\mu,\nu\},\{f,\rho,\sigma\}}\to\sum_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}[\Omega^{ab}_{e}]_{\mu\mu^{\prime}}[\Omega^{ec}_{d}]_{\nu\nu^{\prime}}[(F^{abc}_{d})_{\{x\}}]_{\{e,\mu,\nu^{\prime}\},\{f,\rho^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\}}[\Omega^{bc}_{f}]_{\rho^{\prime}\rho}^{-1}[\Omega^{af}_{d}]_{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}^{-1},

(216)

\displaystyle[(R^{ab}_{c})_{\{x\}}]_{\mu\nu}\to\sum_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}[\Omega^{ba}_{c}]_{\mu\mu^{\prime}}[(R^{ab}_{c})_{\{x\}}]_{\mu\nu}[\Omega^{ab}_{c}]_{\nu^{\prime}\nu}^{-1},

\displaystyle[U_{g}(a,b;c)_{12}]_{\mu\nu}\to\sum_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}K^{q(g)}[\Omega^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}]_{\mu\mu^{\prime}}K^{q(g)}[U_{g}(a,b;c)_{12}]_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}[\Omega^{ab}_{c}]_{\nu^{\prime}\nu}^{-1}\times\frac{\delta^{a}_{g}\delta^{b}_{g}}{\delta^{c}_{g}},

\displaystyle\eta_{a}(g,h)_{1}\to\frac{\delta^{a}_{gh}}{\delta^{a}_{g}K^{q(g)}\delta^{a}_{h}K^{q(g)}}\eta_{a}(g,h),

where
\Omega^{ab}_{c}=e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21}
and
\delta^{a}_{g}=\frac{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(g)}}{e^{i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}}
, then the primed data
U_{g}^{\prime}(a,b;c)
and
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)
transform in the same form as the gauge transformation in
G
-BTC:

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)\to K^{q^{\prime}(g)}\left(\Omega^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a)\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(b)}_{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(c)}\right)K^{q^{\prime}(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)\left(\Omega^{ab}_{c}\right)^{-1}\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{g}\Delta^{b}_{g}}{\Delta^{c}_{g}},

(217)

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\to\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{gh}}{\Delta^{a}_{g}K^{q^{\prime}(g)}(\Delta_{h}^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a)})K^{q^{\prime}(g)}},

where

\displaystyle\Omega^{ab}_{c}=e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21},

(218)

\displaystyle\Delta^{a}_{g}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}&\textrm{when $g$ preserves spatial orientation,}\\
K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}\right)K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}&\textrm{when $g$ reverses spatial orientation.}\end{array}\right.

To prove the statement, we first note that, according to the definition of
A
matrices, they should transform under the vertex basis gauge transformation as,

A^{\bar{b}\bar{a}}_{\bar{c}}\to\frac{\Omega^{\bar{a}a}_{0}\Omega^{\bar{b}b}_{0}}{\Omega^{\bar{c}c}_{0}}\left(\Omega^{ab}_{c}\right)^{*}A^{\bar{b}\bar{a}}_{\bar{c}}\left(\Omega^{\bar{b}\bar{a}}_{\bar{c}}\right)^{-1},

(219)

with
\Omega^{ab}_{c}=e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21}]
. It is straightforward to show that the transformation of
A
, together with Eq. (
216
) keeps the equations Eqs. (
187
)-(
195
) invariant.

When the symmetry
g
preserves spatial orientation, the CEP map is an identity map, and it is straightforward that

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)=U_{g}(a,b;c)\to K^{q(g)}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)_{21}]\right)K^{q(g)}\times

\displaystyle[U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)]\left(e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21}]\right)^{-1}\times K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\varphi^{c}_{g}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}e^{i\varphi^{b}_{g}(x_{1})}},

When the symmetry action
g
reverses spatial orientation, we find that

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)=K^{q(g)}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)\to

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\frac{\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}\right)\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};0)_{21}\right)}{\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{c},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c};0)_{21}\right)}\left(e^{i\phi_{12}^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})_{21}\right)^{*}A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}\left(e^{i\phi_{12}^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)_{21}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)_{21}]\right)([R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}])\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)_{21}]\right)^{-1}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb;^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc)_{21}]\right)K^{q(g)}\times

\displaystyle[U_{g}(a,b;c)]\left(e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21}]^{-1}\right)\times K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\varphi^{c}_{g}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}e^{i\varphi^{b}_{g}(x_{1})}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\left(e^{i\phi_{12}^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})_{21}\right)^{*}K^{q(g)}\times K^{q(g)}(A^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc})K^{q(g)}U_{g}(a,b;c)\times\left(e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21}]^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}\frac{\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}\right)\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};0)_{21}\right)}{\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{c},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c};0)_{21}\right)}K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\varphi^{c}_{g}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}e^{i\varphi^{b}_{g}(x_{1})}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{q(g)}\left(e^{i\phi_{12}^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c})_{21}\right)^{*}K^{q(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)\left(e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21}]^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptb}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}{e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0ptc}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}}\frac{\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}\right)\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{b},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{b};0)_{21}\right)}{\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{c},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{c};0)_{21}\right)}K^{q(g)}\frac{e^{i\varphi^{c}_{g}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}e^{i\varphi^{b}_{g}(x_{1})}}.

Summarizing the above two cases, we find that, under the transformations in Eq. (
216
),

U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)\to K^{q(g)+p(g)}\left(e^{i\phi_{12}^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(b)}}\Omega_{0}(\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a),\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(b);\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(c))_{21}\right)K^{q(g)+p(g)}U^{\prime}_{g}(a,b;c)\left(e^{i\phi^{b}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(a,b;c)_{21}]^{-1}\right)\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{g}\Delta^{b}_{g}}{\Delta^{c}_{g}},

(220)

where

\Delta^{a}_{g}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}&\textrm{when $g$ preserves spatial orientation,}\\
K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e_{12}^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}}\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}\right)K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}&\textrm{when $g$ reverses spatial orientation.}\end{array}\right.

This transformation is precisely the gauge transformation for
U
-symbols in
G
-BTC.

For the
\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)
-phases, when both
g
and
h
reverse spatial orientation, we check that

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)=\eta_{a}(g,h)K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{0})^{-1}K^{q(g)}\to

\displaystyle\frac{K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(x_{1})}K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{g}^{a}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}\eta_{a}(g,h)\times

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)_{21}]\right)^{-1}\times\frac{K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}}{e^{i\varphi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{h}(x_{1})}e^{i\varphi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{h}(x_{1})}}\times

\displaystyle\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)^{-1}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{0})^{-1}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=\frac{K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(x_{1})}K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{g}^{a}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}e^{-i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}\eta_{a}(g,h)\times

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)^{-1}(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{0})^{-1}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=\eta_{a}(g,h)K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)(R^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{0})^{-1}K^{q(g)}\times\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}e^{-i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi_{g}^{a}(x_{1})}}\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{q(g)}\left(K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a},^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta;0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(h)}e^{-i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(x_{1})}\right)^{*}K^{q(g)}}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{gh}}{\Delta^{a}_{g}K^{q(g)}(\Delta_{h}^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a)})^{*}K^{q(g)}}.

When
g
reverses spatial orientation while
h
preserves spatial orientation (keep in mind that
gh
reverses spatial orientation),

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)=\eta_{a}(g,h)K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}\to

\displaystyle\frac{K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(x_{1})}K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{g}^{a}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}\eta_{a}(g,h)\times

\displaystyle K^{q(gh)}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(h)}U_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)^{-1}\times\frac{K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}}{e^{i\varphi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{h}(x_{1})}e^{i\varphi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{h}(x_{1})}}K^{q(g)}

\displaystyle=\eta_{a}(g,h)K^{q(g)}U_{h}(^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)K^{q(g)}\times\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(gh)}e^{-i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi_{g}^{a}(x_{1})}}\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{q(g)}\left(K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}e^{-i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}(x_{1})}\right)^{*}K^{q(g)}}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{gh}}{\Delta^{a}_{g}K^{q(g)}(\Delta_{h}^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a)})^{*}K^{q(g)}}.

When
g
preserves spatial orientation while
h
reverses spatial orientation,

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)=\eta_{a}(g,h)\to

\displaystyle\frac{K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(x_{1})}K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{g}^{a}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}\eta_{a}(g,h)

\displaystyle=\eta_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]\right)K^{q(gh)}e^{-i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}{K^{q(g)}\left(K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}\left(e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a}}_{12}}[\Omega_{0}(^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta,^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt\bar{a};0)_{21}]K^{q(h)}\right)e^{i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(x_{1})}\right){K^{q(g)}}\left(K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}\right)}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{gh}}{\Delta^{a}_{g}K^{q(g)}(\Delta_{h}^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a)})K^{q(g)}}.

Finally, when
g
and
h
both preserve spatial orientation, it is straightforward that

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)=\eta_{a}(g,h)\to

\displaystyle\frac{K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(x_{1})}K^{q(g)}e^{i\varphi_{g}^{a}(x_{1})}}{e^{i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}{K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}}\eta_{a}(g,h)

\displaystyle=\eta_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{K^{q(gh)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(gh)}e^{-i\varphi_{gh}^{a}(x_{1})}}{K^{q(g)}\left(K^{q(h)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\overline{gh}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\overline{h}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}e^{i\varphi_{h}^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}(x_{1})}\right){K^{q(g)}}\left(K^{q(g)}e^{i\phi^{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pta}_{{}^{\bar{g}}\hskip-1.0pt11}}K^{q(g)}e^{-i\varphi^{a}_{g}(x_{1})}\right)}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{gh}}{\Delta^{a}_{g}K^{q(g)}(\Delta_{h}^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a)})K^{q(g)}}.

Summarizing the above four cases, under the transformations Eq. (
216
), the
\eta^{\prime}
-phases transform as

\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\to\eta^{\prime}_{a}(g,h)\times\frac{\Delta^{a}_{gh}}{\Delta^{a}_{g}K^{q(g)+p(g)}(\Delta_{h}^{\rho^{\prime}_{\bar{g}}(a)})K^{q(g)+p(g)}},

(221)

which is precisely the symmetry action gauge transformation in
G
-BTC.

H.4
Emergent RT symmetry is not directly related to crystalline equivalence principle

In constructing the map Eq. (
39
), the emergent CRT symmetry of the TQSLs play a crucial role. As discussed in Sec.
V
, all TQSLs also have an emergent RT symmetry. But unlike the emergent CRT symmetry, this RT symmetry is not directly related to the crystalline equivalence principle. This fact is also why
\rho^{\prime}_{g}(a)
in Eq. (
39
) takes that form. We finish this appendix by giving an intuitive explanation of this point.

Concretely, consider an example of symmetry-protected topological (SPT) phase with a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
internal symmetry and a reflection symmetry
R
, which commute with each other. In the effective field theory of this SPT phase, denote by
\psi
the field that carries a unit
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
charge, i.e., under the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
and
R
symmetries,
\psi
transforms as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{Z}_{4}:\psi(x)\rightarrow i\psi(x),\\
&R:\psi(x)\rightarrow\psi(^{R}x).\end{aligned}

(222)

Suppose that this SPT phase is related to an SPT phase with a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
internal symmetry and a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
time reversal symmetry, via the crystalline equivalence principle. The crystalline equivalence principle asserts that this
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetry should commute with the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
internal symmetry. So if we denote by
\psi^{\prime}
the field that carries a unit
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
charge in this new SPT, then it transforms under the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetry as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{Z}_{4}:\psi^{\prime}(x)\rightarrow i\psi^{\prime}(x),\\
&\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}:\psi^{\prime}(x)\rightarrow\psi^{\prime\dagger}(x),\end{aligned}

(223)

where the hermitian conjugate in the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
action is needed for the actions of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
to commute. Note that this
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
is often considered as the CT symmetry in the field theoretic context, rather than the T symmetry. This observation already suggests that it is CRT, rather than RT, that is the building block of the crystalline equivalence principle.

Now suppose that we gauge the internal
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry, which turns the above two SPT phases into a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
topological order with a reflection symmetry
R
and a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
topological order with a time reversal symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
. These two
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
topological orders should still be related by the crystalline equivalence principle. From the actions of
R
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
in the two SPT phases, we see that the anyon permutation patterns in these two gauged theories should be related as in Eq. (
39
).

Appendix I
Symmetry-enriched
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
TQSLs

In this appendix, we give more details on symmetry-enriched
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
TQSLs discussed in Sec.
VI
. We will explain the physical meanings of
\eta^{\prime}_{e}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\eta^{\prime}_{m}(g_{1},g_{2})
, and explain how to obtain the expansion coefficients in Eqs. (
62
), (
73
) and (
85
).

I.1
p4m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetric
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
TQSLs

We start with
p4m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetric
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
TQSLs, where the symmetries do not permute the anyons. These TQSLs are characterized by the fractionalization pattern of the
p4m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetry on the
e
and
m
anyons, which, mathematically, are characterized by an element in
\mathcal{H}^{2}(p4m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})

Ye and Zou 2024
. The physical meanings of
\eta^{\prime}_{e}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\eta^{\prime}_{m}(g_{1},g_{2})
can be read off using the following 20 “cohomology invariants”:

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{2},C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1}T_{2}C_{2},T_{1}T_{2}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1}C_{2},T_{1}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(M,M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1}M,T_{1}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},

(224)

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{4}M,C_{4}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},T_{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{4},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},C_{4})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(M,\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},M)},\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}),

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{2},C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1}T_{2}C_{2},T_{1}T_{2}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1}C_{2},T_{1}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(M,M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1}M,T_{1}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{4}M,C_{4}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},T_{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{4},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},C_{4})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(M,\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},M)},\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}).

These 20 quantities are invariant under the coboundary transformations relevant to
\mathcal{H}^{2}(p4m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
, and their values completely characterize an element in this cohomology. They are also listed in Table XXVI of Ref.
Ye and Zou 2024
. The meaning of each of them can be easily speculated from its definition. For example, roughly speaking,
\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{2},C_{2})/\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})=1
and
\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{2},C_{2})/\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})=-1
mean that acting
C_{2}
twice on
e
results in a
1
and
-1
phase factor, respectively,
\frac{\eta_{m}(T_{1},\mathcal{T})}{\eta_{m}(\mathcal{T},T_{1})}=1
and
\frac{\eta_{m}(T_{1},\mathcal{T})}{\eta_{m}(\mathcal{T},T_{1})}=-1
mean that
T_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}
commute and anti-commute on
m
, respectively, and
\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})=1
and
\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})=-1
mean that
m
is a Kramers doublet under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
.

To get Eq. (
62
) for the toric code, we first use our crystalline equivalence principle Eq. (
39
) to get
\eta^{\prime}_{e}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\eta^{\prime}_{m}(g_{1},g_{2})
, based on which we can calculate the invariants in Eq. (
224
). The results are

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{2},C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1}T_{2}C_{2},T_{1}T_{2}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1}C_{2},T_{1}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(M,M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1}M,T_{1}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,

(225)

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{4}M,C_{4}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},T_{1})}=-1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{4},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},C_{4})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(M,\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},M)}=1,\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})=1,

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{2},C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1}T_{2}C_{2},T_{1}T_{2}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1}C_{2},T_{1}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(M,M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1}M,T_{1}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{4}M,C_{4}M)}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})}=1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},T_{1})}=-1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{4},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},C_{4})}=-1,\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(M,\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},M)}=-1,\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})=1.

On the other hand, one can calculate the invariants in Eq. (
224
) for each entry in Eq. (
60
). By matching the obtained values with Eq. (
225
), we can get Eq. (
62
). To finish this calculation, we need the definitions of each entry in Eq. (
60
), which is reproduced from Appendix B. 4 in Ref.
Ye and Zou 2024
:

\displaystyle B_{xy}(g_{1},g_{2})=P_{c}(c_{1})y_{1}x_{2}+P(c_{1})y_{2}(y_{1}+x_{2}),

(226)

\displaystyle B_{c^{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\frac{1}{4}\left([c_{1}]_{4}+(-1)^{m_{1}}[c_{2}]_{4}-[c_{1}+(-1)^{m_{1}}c_{2}]_{4}\right),

\displaystyle A^{2}_{x+y}(g_{1},g_{2})=(x_{1}+y_{1})(x_{2}+y_{2}),

\displaystyle A_{x+y}A_{m}(g_{1},g_{2})=m_{1}(x_{2}+y_{2}),

\displaystyle A_{c}^{2}=c_{1}c_{2},\quad A_{m}^{2}=m_{1}m_{2},\quad A_{c}t=c_{1}t_{2},

\displaystyle A_{x+y}t=(x_{1}+y_{1})t_{2},\quad A_{m}t=m_{1}t_{2},\quad t^{2}=t_{1}t_{2},

where we have labeled each group element as
g=T_{1}^{x}T_{2}^{y}C_{4}^{c}M^{m}\mathcal{T}^{t}
with
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}
,
c\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, and
m,t\in\{0,1\}
. The functions
P
,
P_{c}
, and
[x]_{a}
are

\displaystyle P(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&x\text{ is odd,}\\
0,&x\text{ is even,}\end{array}\right.\quad P_{c}(x)=1-P(x),

(227)

\displaystyle[x]_{a}=\{y=x\;(\text{mod }a)|0\leqslant y<x\}.

Similar analysis also leads to Eq. (
73
) for the flipped toric code.

I.2
p6m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetric
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
TQSLs

Next, we turn to
p6m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetric
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
TQSLs, where the symmetries do not permute anyons. These TQSLs are characterized by the fractionalization pattern of the
p6m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetry on the
e
and
m
anyons, which, mathematically, are characterized by an elment in
\mathcal{H}^{2}(p6m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})

Ye and Zou 2024
. The physical meanings of
\eta^{\prime}_{e}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\eta^{\prime}_{m}(g_{1},g_{2})
can be read off from the following 14 “cohomology invariants”:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{2},C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(T_{1}C_{2},T_{1}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{2}M,C_{2}M)\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}),\eta^{\prime}_{e}(M,M)\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}),\\
&\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(C_{6},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},C_{6})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(M,\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},M)},\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{e}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}),\\
&\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{2},C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(T_{1}C_{2},T_{1}C_{2})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})},\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{2}M,C_{2}M)\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}),\eta^{\prime}_{m}(M,M)\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}),\\
&\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(C_{6},\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},C_{6})},\frac{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(M,\mathcal{T})}{\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},M)},\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T})\eta^{\prime}_{m}(\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}).\end{aligned}

(228)

These 14 quantities are invariant under the coboundary transformations relevant to
\mathcal{H}^{2}(p6m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
, and their values completely characterize an element in this cohomology. The meaning of each of them can be similarly read off, as in the case of
p4m\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{T}
symmetric
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
TQSLs.

To get Eq. (
85
), we first use our crystalline equivalence principle Eq. (
39
) to get
\eta^{\prime}_{e}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\eta^{\prime}_{m}(g_{1},g_{2})
, based on which we can calculate the invariants in Eq. (
228
). We find that all 14 invariants are 1 in this case, which means that we can take
\vec{\chi}^{e}=\vec{\chi}^{m}=\vec{0}
.

For completeness, we reproduce the definition of the 2-cocycles in Eq. (
83
) from Appendix B.2 in Ref.
Ye and Zou 2024
.

\displaystyle B_{xy}(g_{1},g_{2})=P_{60}[P_{c}(m_{1})y_{1}x_{2}+m_{1}y_{2}(x_{2}+y_{1})]

(229)

\displaystyle+P_{61}\left[P_{c}(m_{1})\left(\frac{x_{2}(x_{2}-1)}{2}+y_{1}x_{2}-y_{2}(x_{2}+y_{1})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+m_{1}\left(\frac{y_{2}(y_{2}-1)}{2}+y_{1}(y_{2}-x_{2})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+P_{62}\left[P_{c}(m_{1})\left(\frac{y_{2}(y_{2}-1)}{2}-x_{2}-y_{2}(x_{2}+y_{1})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+m_{1}\left(\frac{x_{2}(x_{2}-1)}{2}-y_{2}-y_{1}x_{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+P_{63}[P_{c}(m_{1})(y_{2}-x_{2}-y_{1}x_{2})+m_{1}(x_{2}-y_{2}+y_{2}(x_{2}-y_{1})]

\displaystyle+P_{64}\left[P_{c}(m_{1})\left(\frac{x_{2}(x_{2}-1)}{2}+y_{2}-y_{1}x_{2}-y_{2}(x_{2}-y_{1})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+m_{1}\left(\frac{y_{2}(y_{2}-1)}{2}+x_{2}+y_{1}(x_{2}-y_{2})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+P_{65}\left[P_{c}(m_{1})\left(\frac{y_{2}(y_{2}-1)}{2}-y_{2}(x_{2}-y_{1})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+m_{1}\left(\frac{x_{2}(x_{2}-1)}{2}+y_{1}x_{2}\right)\right],

\displaystyle A_{c}A_{m}(g_{1},g_{2})=m_{1}c_{2},\quad A_{c}^{2}=c_{1}c_{2},

\displaystyle A_{m}^{2}=m_{1}m_{2},\quad A_{c}t=c_{1}t_{2},

\displaystyle A_{m}t=m_{1}t_{2},\quad t^{2}=t_{1}t_{2}.

where we have labeled each group element as
g=T_{1}^{x}T_{2}^{y}C_{6}^{c}M^{m}\mathcal{T}^{t}
with
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}
,
c\in\{0,1,2,3,4,5\}
, and
m,t\in\{0,1\}
. The function
P_{c}
is defined in Eq. (
227
) and
P_{ab}
is defined by

\displaystyle P_{ab}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&x=b\text{ mod }a,\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(230)
